\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{i_{1}a_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{i_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}}\bigg[\sum_{j_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{j_{1}a_{2}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{j_{1},(b,c)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{i_{1}a_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{i_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{a_{2},(b,c)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{a_{2},(b,c)}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{i_{1}a_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{i_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{a_{2},(b,c)}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{a_{1},(b,c)}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\bigg)_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\bigg)_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{2})_{a_{1}a_{2}},

where we have used the fact that
\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}=\mathbf{T}_{1}
and the fact that
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}=({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}
. This calculation reveals that
\mathbf{I}_{1}
is simply a diagonal matrix with repeated diagonal blocks of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{2}
, with a similar observation holding for
\mathbf{I}_{2}
and
\mathbf{I}_{3}
, with diagonal blocks equal to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{2}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{2}
respectively.

Finally, we note that from (
56
) that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{\partial}{\partial({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{l_{1}a_{1}}}

\displaystyle\log f(\mathcal{\widehat{T}};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3},\mathcal{C})\frac{\partial}{\partial({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{l_{2}a_{2}}}\log f(\mathcal{\widehat{T}};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3},\mathcal{C})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{3}}\bigg(\sum_{b,c}\mathcal{C}_{a_{1}bc}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{l_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{a,c}\mathcal{C}_{ab_{1}c}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{l_{1}a}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg).

Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{b,c}\mathcal{C}_{a_{1}bc}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{l_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{l_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg(\sum_{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{j_{1}a_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{j_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{j_{3}c}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{l_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\bigg)_{a_{1},(b,c)}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{T}_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}\bigg)_{a_{1},(l_{2},i_{3})}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)_{a_{1},(l_{2},i_{3})}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})_{a_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},a_{1}}.

By a similar argument,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{a,c}\mathcal{C}_{ab_{1}c}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{l_{1}a}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg)

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})_{b_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{2}\big)_{(l_{1}-1)p_{2}+i_{3}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{3}}

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{b,c}\mathcal{C}_{a_{1}bc}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{l_{2}b}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{a,c}\mathcal{C}_{ab_{1}c}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{l_{1}a}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{i_{3}c}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{3}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})_{b_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{2}\big)_{(l_{1}-1)p_{2}+i_{3}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})_{a_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},a_{1}},

(58)

with appropriate replacements for different modes.

•

Step 3: Inverting the Fisher Information Matrix
:
Recall that we have that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{1}&0&0\\
0&\mathbf{I}_{2}&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{I}_{3}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathbf{H}_{12}&\mathbf{H}_{13}\\
\mathbf{H}_{12}^{\top}&0&\mathbf{H}_{23}\\
\mathbf{H}_{13}^{\top}&\mathbf{H}_{23}^{\top}&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}+\mathcal{H}.

It is straightforward to observe that the matrix
\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}
is invertible since it is a diagonal matrix with positive elements. We note also that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}\|

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathbf{H}_{12}&\mathbf{H}_{13}\\
\mathbf{H}_{12}^{\top}&0&\mathbf{H}_{23}\\
\mathbf{H}_{13}^{\top}&\mathbf{H}_{23}^{\top}&0\end{pmatrix}\Bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\max_{jk}\|\mathbf{H}_{jk}\|

We calculate the maximum spectral norm of each block. Note that by (
58
),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{12}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{H}_{12}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\sum_{l_{1},l_{2},a_{1},b_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{i_{3}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})_{b_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{2}\big)_{(l_{1}-1)p_{2}+i_{3}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})_{a_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},a_{1}}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\sum_{l_{1},l_{2},a_{1},b_{1},i_{3}}\bigg((\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})_{b_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{2}\big)_{(l_{1}-1)p_{2}+i_{3}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg((\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})_{a_{1}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},a_{1}}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\Bigg[\sum_{l_{1},l_{2},i_{3}}\|\big(\mathbf{V}_{2}\big)_{(l_{1}-1)p_{2}+i_{3},\cdot}\|^{2}\|\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},\cdot}\|^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\Bigg[\frac{\mu_{0}^{2}r}{p_{2}p_{3}}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2},i_{3}}\|\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},\cdot}\|^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\Bigg[\frac{C\mu_{0}^{2}r}{p}\sum_{l_{2},i_{3}}\|\big(\mathbf{V}_{1}\big)_{(l_{2}-1)p_{3}+i_{3},\cdot}\|^{2}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\big(\frac{C\mu_{0}^{2}r}{p}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}.

Therefore, applying this argument to each block, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\|\|\mathcal{H}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathcal{H}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}

\displaystyle\ll 1,

provided that
\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}\ll 1
.

Therefore, the following series expansion is justified:

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}\mathcal{I})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mathcal{I}+\mathcal{H}\bigg)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}\bigg(\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\bigg)\Bigg]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\bigg)^{-1}\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\big)^{k},

and hence that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\big)^{k}\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}+\sigma^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\big)^{k}\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}(1-o(1))\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1},

where the
o(1)
is taken to be with respect to the positive semidefinite ordering.

•

Step 4: Putting It All Together
: First we prove
Theorem

4
. It is immediate that the
m
’th row of any estimator
\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{k}
of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
has covariance lower bounded by the
r_{k}\times r_{k}
submatrix of
\mathcal{I}^{-1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{k})

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\big(\mathcal{I}^{-1}\big)_{r_{k}:r_{k}}

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\sigma^{2}(1-o(1))\big(\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\big)_{r_{k}:r_{k}}

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\sigma^{2}(1-o(1))\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-2},

since the
r_{k}\times r_{k}
submatrix of
\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}
corresponding to the
m
’th row of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
is simply
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-2}
. This completes the proof of
Theorem

4
.

We now complete the proof of
Theorem

8
. We have that

\displaystyle\big(

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big)^{\top}\mathcal{I}^{-1}\big(\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big)

\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\big(\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big)^{\top}\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-1}\big(\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big).

Note that from (
54
) and (
57
) that the vector

\displaystyle\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}

is only nonzero in indices corresponding to rows
i

j
or
k
. Each of these is then scaled by the diagonal matrix
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-2}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{-2}
, or
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}^{-3}
respectively.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big)^{\top}\mathcal{I}^{-1}\big(\nabla_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\big)

\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})_{s_{1},(j-1)p_{3}+k}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})_{s_{2},(j-1)p_{3}+k}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})_{s_{1},(k-1)p_{1}+i}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})_{s_{2},(k-1)p_{1}+i}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top})_{s_{1},(i-1)p_{2}+j}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{2}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top})_{s_{2},(i-1)p_{2}+j}

\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})_{s_{1},(j-1)p_{3}+k}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})_{s_{2},(j-1)p_{3}+k}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})_{s_{1},(k-1)p_{1}+i}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})_{s_{1},(k-1)p_{1}+i}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top})_{s_{1},(i-1)p_{2}+j}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3})^{-2}_{s_{1}s_{1}}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3}\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top})_{s_{2},(i-1)p_{2}+j}

\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})_{s_{1},(j-1)p_{3}+k}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top})_{s_{1},(k-1)p_{1}+i}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\sum_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top})_{s_{1},(i-1)p_{2}+j}^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\sigma^{2}\bigg(\|e_{(j-1)p_{3}+k}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|^{2}+\|e_{(k-1)p_{2}+j}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|^{2}+\|e_{(i-1)p_{2}+j}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|^{2}\bigg).

This completes the proof of
Theorem

8
.

∎

G.2
Proofs of
Theorems

5
and
9

In this section we prove the minimax lower bounds for the length of the confidence intervals.

G.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

5

Proof of
Theorem

5
.

Without loss of generality we may assume that
\|\xi\|_{\infty}=\sigma_{\min}=1
, since the result is invariant to rescaling by these quantities. We further assume that
k=m=1
. Our proof mimics the proof of Theorem 3 of
Cai and Guo (2017)
, but our construction is inspired by a similar construction in
Cheng et al. (2021)
.

Take any
\mathcal{T}
such that
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{C}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\frac{\mu_{0}}{2},\sigma,\sigma_{\min})
, and suppose
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
is such that
\|\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{\cdot,r_{k}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{0}
denote the hypothesis space with point mass at
\mathcal{T}
, and let
\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}
denote the prior on this set. We will construct an alternative hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}_{1}
with prior given point mass
\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}
of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}=\mathcal{C}\times_{1}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{U}}_{3},

where
\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}
is an orthonormal matrix satisfying certain properties. By Lemma 1 of
Cai and Guo (2017)
and properties of the infimum, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathrm{C.I.}_{k,m}^{\alpha}(\xi,\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\in\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\Theta,\xi)}\sup_{\mathcal{T}\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{T}}L\big(\mathrm{C.I.}_{k,m}^{\alpha}(\xi,\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\mathrm{C.I.}_{k,m}^{\alpha}(\xi,\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\in\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\Theta,\xi)}\sup_{\mathcal{T}\in\Theta_{0}\cup\Theta_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{T}}L\big(\mathrm{C.I.}_{k,m}^{\alpha}(\xi,\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\big|\xi^{\top}\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}-\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}\big|\big(1-2\alpha-\mathrm{TV}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})\big),

where
\mathrm{TV}(\cdot)
denotes the total variation distance, and
f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}}
denotes the density function with the prior
\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}
on
\mathcal{H}_{i}
. We note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})},

where

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{1},f_{0})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{f_{1}^{2}(z)}{f_{0}(z)}dz-1.

We will upper bound
\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})
and lower bound
\big|\xi^{\top}\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}-\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}\big|
.

First we describe our construction. For a given
\mathbf{U}_{1}
, let
\mathbf{U^{\prime}}_{1}
be defined as follows. Let
\mathbf{v}
denote the vector

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}:=\frac{1}{\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|}\bigg(({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\bigg),

where we will choose
\delta
later. We set
\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}
to have the first
r_{1}-1
columns equal to
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
, and we let its
r_{1}
’th column equal
\mathbf{v}
. The proof proceeds in steps: first we demonstrate that
\mathcal{\bar{T}}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0},\sigma,\sigma_{\min})
, after which we upper bound the
\chi^{2}
distance, and finally we lower bound the difference
\big|\xi^{\top}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}-\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}\big|
.

•

Step 1: Showing
\mathcal{\bar{T}}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0},\sigma,\sigma_{\min}).
It is clear that the singular values and the singular vectors
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
of
\mathcal{\bar{T}}
remain unchanged; consequently, we need only demonstrate that
\|\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}
. Therefore, it suffices to demonstrate that
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}
. First, note that

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta((\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}))e_{1}\|=\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|((\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}))e_{1}\|^{2}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}-\mathbf{v}\|_{\infty}+\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}\big)_{\cdot r_{1}}-\frac{({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}\bigg\|+\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta\big(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\big)e_{1}\bigg\|_{\infty}\bigg|1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}\bigg|+\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\delta\bigg)\bigg|1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}\bigg|+\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\delta\bigg)+\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}.

Therefore,
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}
as long as

\displaystyle\delta\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\delta\bigg)\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}.

(59)

We will check this upon choosing
\delta
.

•

Step 2: Upper bounding
\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})
. Under Gaussian noise, we have that

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})+1

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg(\sum_{i,j,k}\frac{(\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}-\mathcal{T}_{ijk})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{ijk}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\|\mathcal{\bar{T}}-\mathcal{T}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\lambda_{r_{1}}^{2}\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}-\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\bigg),

where we have used the assumption
\sigma_{\min}=1
.
We now bound the quantity inside the exponential. Observe that

\displaystyle\|\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}\big)_{\cdot r_{1}}-\mathbf{v}\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}\big)_{\cdot r_{1}}-\frac{({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta\bigg\|\bigg(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)e_{1}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|}}\bigg)\|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{\cdot r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta+\frac{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{i}\|}-1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}(1+\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta,

where the final inequality holds as long as
\delta\leq\frac{1}{12}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(9\lambda_{r_{1}}^{2}\delta^{2}\bigg)-1.

•

Step 3: Lower bounding
\xi^{\top}(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}
.

Without loss of generality assume that
({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}
is nonnegative. Observe that

\displaystyle|\xi^{\top}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}|

\displaystyle=|\xi_{r_{1}}|\bigg|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq C\|\xi\|_{\infty}\bigg|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq C\|\xi\|_{\infty}\bigg|\mathbf{v}_{11}-\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=C\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}\bigg(({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}+\delta(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\bigg)-\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1r_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq C\Bigg(\frac{\delta\||(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})e_{1}\|^{2}}}-({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{11}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|^{2}}}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq C\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\delta\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|-|({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{11}|\delta^{2}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|\bigg)^{\top}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C\delta}{4}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|.

The incoherence assumption implies that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}

and hence

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})_{11}\|\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\xi^{\top}(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C\delta}{8}.

•

Completing the proof
. Combining all of our bounds, we obtain that

\displaystyle\inf\sup\mathbb{E}L\big(\mathrm{C.I.}_{1,1}^{\alpha}(\xi,\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\geq\big|\xi^{\top}\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}-\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{1\cdot}\big|\big(1-2\alpha-\mathrm{TV}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C\delta}{9}\bigg(1-2\alpha-\sqrt{\exp\bigg(9\lambda_{r_{1}}^{2}\delta^{2}\bigg)-1}\bigg).

By taking
\delta=\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda_{r_{1}}}
for some sufficiently small constant
\varepsilon
, we complete the proof, provided we can justify (
59
). However, with this choice of
\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\delta\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\delta\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda_{r_{k}}}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda_{r_{k}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda/\sigma}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda/\sigma}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{0}\sqrt{p_{1}}}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{0}\sqrt{p_{1}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{0}\sqrt{p_{1}}}\bigg(1+\frac{2\varepsilon}{\mu_{0}C_{0}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p_{1}}},

where we have used the assumption that
\lambda/\sigma\geq C_{0}\sqrt{p_{\max}}
together with the fact that
\sigma\geq 1
from the fact that
\sigma_{\min}=1
.

This completes the proof.

∎

G.2.2
Proof of
Theorem

9

Proof of
Theorem

9
.

The proof is similar to the previous proof, only we generalize our construction slightly. Without loss of generality we consider
i=j=k=1
. First let
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{C}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
be such that
\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}
for each
k
which is permitted since
\mu_{0}>2
. Let
\mathcal{T}
also satisfy

\displaystyle\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|^{2}}\geq C\mu_{0}\frac{r_{\max}^{3/2}\kappa}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{\min}}},

which is possible whenever
\lambda\geq C_{0}\sqrt{p}r\kappa
, which holds under our assumptions on the class
\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0},\sigma,\sigma_{\min})
. Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}=\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\bigg)\Bigg[\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\bigg)\Bigg]^{-1/2},

where
\Delta_{k}
is the
p_{k}\times r_{k}
matrix whose first row has
l
’th entry equal to
\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda_{l}}
for some constant
\varepsilon
to be determined later, and whose sign will be chosen later. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}:=\mathcal{C}\times_{1}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}.

By Lemma 1 of
Cai and Guo (2017)
, we have that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathrm{C.I.}^{\alpha}_{111}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\in\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\Theta,\{1,1,1\}}\sup_{\mathcal{T}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{T}}L\big(\mathrm{C.I.}_{111}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\big)

\displaystyle\geq|\mathcal{T}_{ijk}-\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}|\big(1-2\alpha-\sqrt{\chi^{2}(f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}},f_{\pi_{{\mathcal{H}_{0}}}})}\big).

Similar to the previous proof, we proceed in steps.

•

Step 1: Checking that
\mathcal{\bar{T}}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0})
. It is evident that
\lambda\leq\lambda_{\min}(\mathcal{\bar{T}})\leq\lambda_{\max}(\mathcal{\bar{T}})\leq\kappa\lambda
. Therefore, it suffices to demonstrate that
\|\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\big)\Delta_{k}+\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\big)\Delta_{k}\bigg)\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}\bigg),

(60)

where we have defined
\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}
via

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\big)\Delta_{k}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\big)\Delta_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbf{I}+\Delta_{k}^{\top}\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\big)\Delta_{k},

which is positive definite as long as
\|\Delta_{k}^{\top}({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|<1,
which we will demonstrate momentarily. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}+\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}+\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg)\bigg\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}\bigg\|.

(61)

Therefore, it suffices to bound the quantities
\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}
and
\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|
.

–

Bounding
\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}
. First, observe that we can write
\Delta_{k}
via
\Delta_{k}=\varepsilon\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}
, where
\mathbf{v}\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{k}}
is a vector of signs. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\lambda}\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\|_{2,\infty}.

Since
\mathbf{v}
is a vector of signs, the matrix
(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}
is simply the rank one matrix whose columns are the entries of the first row of the matrix
(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})
. Consequently,
\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\|\leq\sqrt{r_{k}}
. Combining these bounds yields that

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda}.

–

Bounding
\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}({\mathbf{I}}-\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{1/2})\|\leq\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}\|\|{\mathbf{I}}-\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{1/2}\|.

By Theorem 6.2 of
Higham (2008)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{I}}-\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}})+1}\|{\mathbf{I}}-\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{k}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})^{2}\Delta_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{\lambda^{2}},

where we have implicitly implied a similar argument to the previous bound. As a result, we see that as long as
1-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, we have that
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}})\geq\frac{1}{2}
by Weyl’s inequality. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|\leq 2\frac{\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{\lambda^{2}}.

Combining our bounds and plugging this inequality into (
61
), we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda}+\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda}\bigg)\frac{2\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda/\sigma}+\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda/\sigma}\bigg)\frac{2\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{(\lambda/\sigma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{C_{0}\sqrt{p_{k}}}+\bigg(\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{C_{0}\sqrt{p_{k}}}\bigg)\frac{2\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{C_{0}^{2}p_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}},

where the final inequality holds for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, the second inequality holds from the fact that
\sigma\geq\sigma_{\min}=1
, and the penultimate inequality holds from the assumption
\lambda/\sigma\geq C_{0}\kappa r_{\max}\sqrt{p_{\max}}
.

•

Step 2: Upper bounding
\chi^{2}(f_{{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}}},f_{{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}}})
Similar to the proof of
Theorem

5
, it holds that

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(\|\mathcal{\bar{T}}-\mathcal{T}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg)-1.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{\bar{T}-\mathcal{T}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{C}\times_{1}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}-\mathcal{C}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{C}\times_{1}(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\times_{2}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathcal{C}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}-{\mathbf{U}}_{2})\times_{3}\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathcal{C}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}-{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}-{\mathbf{U}}_{2})\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{U}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}-{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{U}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{2})^{\top}\|_{F}.

We now consider an upper bound for the first term; the remaining two terms are similar. From (
60
) we have that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg\|\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{1}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})\Delta_{1}\bigg)\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{1}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\bigg)\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\bigg\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\|}_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\bigg\|({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})\Delta_{1}\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{1}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\bigg)\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\bigg\|}_{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\bigg\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{1}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\bigg)\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}\bigg\|_{F}}_{\alpha_{3}},

where we have passed from the Frobenius norm to the operator norm in the first two terms since the matrix
(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top})\Delta_{k}
is rank one. We now bound
\alpha_{1}
through
\alpha_{3}
.

–

Bounding
\alpha_{1}
.
Recall that
\Delta_{1}=\varepsilon\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}
, where
\mathbf{v}
is a matrix of signs. Therefore,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|\big({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\big)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\big)^{\top}\|.

Next, note that
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3})^{\top}
has the same nonzero singular values as
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
and hence also
\mathcal{T}
. Therefore,
\|\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\big)^{\top}\|=1
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

–

Bounding
\alpha_{2}
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{1}}}{\lambda}\lambda_{1}\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{1}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|\leq\varepsilon\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\kappa r^{3/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\leq\varepsilon\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\kappa r^{3/2}}{(\lambda/\sigma)^{2}}\leq\varepsilon,

where we have used the assumption that
\lambda/\sigma\geq C_{0}r\kappa\sqrt{p}
, together with the previous bounds.

–

Bounding
\alpha_{3}
.
We have that

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{1}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\|\lambda_{1}\sqrt{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\frac{\varepsilon r_{1}^{3/2}\kappa}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\frac{\varepsilon r_{1}^{3/2}\kappa}{\lambda/\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon,

where we have used the assumptions
\lambda/\sigma\geq C_{0}\kappa r\sqrt{p}
and
r_{k}\leq p_{\max}^{1/2}
.

As a consequence, we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{\bar{T}}-\mathcal{T}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 9\varepsilon.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(f_{{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}}},f_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}})\leq\exp\bigg(81\varepsilon^{2}\bigg)-1.

•

Step 3: Lower Bounding
|\mathcal{T}_{ijk}-\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}|
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}-\mathcal{T}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\bigg(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}e_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}e_{1}+\mathbf{v}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top}e_{1}\bigg)\bigg(1+o(1)\bigg),

(62)

where now we choose the sign of
\mathbf{v}_{k}\in\{-1,1\}^{r_{k}}
by taking the sign of the entries of
e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{k}
. For these choices of
\mathbf{v}_{k}
, (
62
) implies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{111}-\mathcal{T}_{111}|

\displaystyle\geq c\varepsilon\bigg(\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\big\|_{1}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\big\|_{1}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\big\|_{1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq c\varepsilon\bigg(\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\big\|_{2}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\big\|_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq c\varepsilon\sqrt{\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\big\|_{2}^{2}}.

(63)

We therefore prove (
62
). Observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}-\mathcal{T}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{l_{3}=1}^{r_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\bigg(\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}-\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}\bigg).

According to (
60
) we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k}+\underbrace{\bigg({\mathbf{U}}_{k}+({\mathbf{I}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top})\Delta_{k}\bigg)\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\Delta_{k}}^{-1/2}-{\mathbf{I}}\bigg)-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\Delta_{k}}_{\Gamma_{k}},

allowing us to write

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{k}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\Delta_{k}+\Gamma_{k}.

From this expression, we have that

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big(\mathbf{\bar{U}}_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}-\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbf{U}_{1}+\Delta_{1}+\Gamma_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}\big(\mathbf{U}_{2}+\Delta_{2}+\Gamma_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big(\mathbf{U}_{3}+\Delta_{3}+\Gamma_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}

\displaystyle\quad-\big({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\big)_{il_{1}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\big)_{jl_{2}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)_{kl_{3}}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{1})_{1l_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{2l_{2}}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{3l_{3}}+({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1l_{1}}\Delta_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}+({\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1l_{1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\Delta_{3}+\mathcal{R}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}},

where
\mathcal{R}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}
contains all of the cross terms with at least two appearances of
\Delta
or at least one appearance of
\Gamma
.

We will demonstrate that
\mathcal{R}
is a lower-order term. First, note that
\Delta_{k}
and
\Gamma_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda};

\displaystyle\|e_{1}^{\top}\Gamma_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda}\bigg)\frac{2\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}}{\lambda^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{r_{k}}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\varepsilon\mu_{0}r_{k}}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{k}}}.

We now bound
\mathcal{R}
directly. There are 8 possible subcases over the possible times
\Delta,\Gamma
, and
{\mathbf{U}}
appear, subject to the fact that
\Delta
must appear with either another
\Delta
or with
\Gamma
, and
{\mathbf{U}}
does not appear three times. Consider, for example the case that
\Delta
appears twice and
{\mathbf{U}}
appears once. In this case we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\big(\Delta_{1}\big)_{1l_{1}}\big(\Delta_{2}\big)_{1l_{2}}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)_{1l_{3}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|e_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{C})\big(\Delta_{1}\otimes\Delta_{2}\big)e_{1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\|e_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\|\lambda_{1}\|e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{1}\|\|e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}\kappa}{\lambda}.

The other possible subcases are all extremely similar, and all satisfy the same upper bound up to some constant by virtue of the bounds on
\|\Gamma_{k}\|_{2,\infty}
and
\|\Delta_{k}\|_{2,\infty}
. As a result, there exists some universal constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\mathcal{R}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}r_{k}\kappa}{\lambda}.

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}-\mathcal{T}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{l_{3}=1}^{r_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\bigg((\Delta_{1})_{1l_{1}}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})_{1l_{2}}({\mathbf{U}}_{3})_{1l_{3}}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1l_{1}}\Delta_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1})_{1l_{1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\Delta_{3}+\mathcal{R}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{3}\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{1})^{\top}e_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}l_{3}}\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}\mathcal{R}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}-\mathcal{T}_{ijk}\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq\bigg|e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{3}\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{1})^{\top}e_{1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad-C\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\mathbf{r}_{k}\kappa}{\lambda}.

Next, recall that the
l
’th entry of the first row of
\Delta_{k}
is of the form
\frac{\pm\varepsilon}{\lambda_{l}}
, and hence

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)^{\top}e_{1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)^{\top}e_{1}=\varepsilon\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}e_{1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}e_{1},

where
\mathbf{v}_{1}
is a
r_{k}
-dimensional vector of signs. Since the signs have not been specified, we are free to select them to match the signs of
e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}
, and hence

\displaystyle\bigg|e_{1}^{\top}

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{2}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}e_{1}+e_{1}^{\top}\Delta_{3}\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{C})({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{1})^{\top}e_{1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon\bigg(\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{1}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{1}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon\bigg(\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}^{2}}.

In addition, we recall that we have assumed that
\mathcal{T}
was selected such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq C\mu_{0}\frac{r_{\max}^{3/2}\kappa}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{\min}}},

which is possible whenever
\lambda\geq C_{0}r_{\max}\kappa
, which holds under the assumption
\lambda\geq C_{0}\kappa\sqrt{p_{\max}}
and the assumption
r_{\max}\leq p_{\min}^{1/2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{ijk}-\mathcal{\bar{T}}_{ijk}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}^{2}}.

•

Step 4: Completing the proof
.
Combining all of our results, we have that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathrm{C.I.}^{\alpha}_{111}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\in\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(\Theta,\{1,1,1\}}\sup_{\mathcal{T}\in\Theta(\lambda,\kappa,\mu_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{T}}L\big(\mathrm{C.I.}_{111}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}^{2}}\big(1-2\alpha-\sqrt{\exp\bigg(81\varepsilon^{2}\bigg)-1}\big)

\displaystyle\geq c\sqrt{\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}+\|e_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{3}\|_{2}^{2}},

provided
\varepsilon
is taken sufficiently small.

This completes the proof.
∎
